<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We construct a separable reflexive real Banach space whose given norm is asymptotically midpoint uniformly convex but which admits no asymptotically uniformly convex equivalent norm. Its averaged midpoint modulus is at least $`\sqrt{1+t^2/12}-1`$, and the countably branching diamond of depth <i>k</i> has distortion at least $`\sqrt{1+k/12}`$ in this space. This gives a negative answer to the reflexive diamond converse for asymptotic uniform convexifiability.

- **C1** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): The space $X=J^*$ defined by <a href="#eq:segment-norm" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:segment-norm">[eq:segment-norm]</a> is infinite dimensional, separable, and reflexive, and has no equivalent AUC norm. Its given norm satisfies $$\widehat\delta_X(t)\ge\sqrt{1+t^2/12}-1\qquad(t>0).$$ For every integer $k\ge0$, every embedding of $D_k$ into $X$ has distortion at least $\sqrt{1+k/12}$.
  - Explanation: The main theorem establishes that the reflexive forest space X admits no equivalent AUC norm, while simultaneously providing an explicit lower bound on its averaged midpoint modulus and on the distortion of countably branching diamonds.
- **C2** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-forest-structure)): The space $X$ defined by <a href="#eq:segment-norm" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:segment-norm">[eq:segment-norm]</a> is infinite dimensional, separable, and reflexive, and the coordinate functionals have dense span in $X$. Let $H\subset\mathcal F$ be finite and *ancestral*, meaning that it contains every ancestor of each of its vertices. The coordinate projections $P_H$ and $Q_H$ are contractive on both $J$ and $X$. Moreover, $P_Hx\to x$ along any increasing ancestral exhaustion of $\mathcal F$.
  - Explanation: Decomposes the space along finite ancestral sets, showing that coordinate projections are contractive on both the predual tree space J and its dual X, while coordinate functionals span a dense subspace.
- **C3** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-cones)): For every finite ancestral $H$, $$\label{eq:cone-decomposition} Q_HJ=\left(\bigoplus_{g\in G_H}J(U_g)\right)_{\ell_2}, \qquad \\|Q_Hx\\|^2=\sum_{g\in G_H}\\|x\|_{J(U_g)}\\|^2\quad(x\in X).$$ The gate-coordinate map $x\mapsto(x(e_g))_{g\in G_H}$ is a contraction from $X$ to $\ell_2(G_H)$.
  - Explanation: Shows that the complement of any finite ancestral set splits into independent descendant cones rooted at minimal exterior gate vertices, with the tail norm decomposing as an ell_2 direct sum.
- **C4** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-modulus-conventions)): For an infinite-dimensional real Banach space $Y$ with norm $N$, let $m_N$ be the modulus obtained from <a href="#eq:average-modulus" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:average-modulus">[eq:average-modulus]</a> by replacing the average by $\max\{N(x+ty),N(x-ty)\}$. Then, for $t>0$, $$0\le\overline\delta_N(t)\le\widehat\delta_N(t) \le m_N(t)\le2\widehat\delta_N(t).$$ For either symmetric modulus the inner infimum is unchanged if $N(y)=1$ is replaced by $N(y)\ge1$. Both symmetric moduli, including their centerwise versions before taking the infimum over $x$, are nondecreasing. Positivity on $(0,1)$ therefore implies positivity at every positive radius.
  - Explanation: Demonstrates the mutual comparability of the one-sided AUC modulus, the averaged AMUC modulus, and the historical maximum AMUC modulus, proving they share identical positivity properties.
- **C5** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-bounded-tree)): Let $Y$ be a real Banach space. Suppose that for every integer $h\ge1$ there are vectors $(u_s^{(h)})_{s\in\mathbb N^{\le h}}$ and constants $m,M,c>0$, independent of $h$, such that $$m\le\\|u_s^{(h)}\\|\le M, \qquad \\|u_{s^\frown j}^{(h)}-u_s^{(h)}\\|\ge c\quad(\|s\|<h).$$ For every nonterminal $s$, suppose the child increments tend weakly to zero as $j\to\infty$. Then every equivalent norm satisfying $\alpha\\|\cdot\\|\le N\le\beta\\|\cdot\\|$, with $0<\alpha\le\beta<\infty$, obeys $$\label{eq:bounded-tree-scale} \overline\delta_N\!\left(\frac{\alpha c}{2\beta M}\right)=0.$$ The same conclusion holds for one infinite tree with these properties.
  - Explanation: Identifies an obstruction to AUC renormability: any Banach space admitting bounded trees whose child increments tend weakly to zero must fail to have an equivalent AUC norm.
- **C6** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-paired)): Let $H\subset\mathcal F$ be finite and ancestral. If $x,y\in X$ satisfy $x=P_Hx$ and $y=Q_Hy$, then $$\label{eq:paired} \frac{\\|x+y\\|+\\|x-y\\|}{2} \ge\sqrt{\\|x\\|^2+\\|y\\|^2/12}.$$ More generally, for all $s,t>0$, $$\label{eq:paired-two-parameters} \frac{\\|x+ty\\|+\\|x-ty\\|}{2} \ge\frac{\\|x\\|+st\\|y\\|}{\sqrt{1+12s^2}}.$$
  - Explanation: Establishes a quantitative lower bound for the symmetric average of an ancestral head and a disjoint tail vector by introducing canceling gate corrections to suppress crossing terms.
- **C7** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-lens)): Let $R>0$, $\varepsilon>0$, $x\in X$, and $(y_j)_{j\ge1}\subset X$ satisfy $$\\|x\pm y_j\\|\le R,\qquad\\|y_i-y_j\\|\ge\varepsilon>0 \quad(i\ne j).$$ Then $$\label{eq:lens} R^2-\\|x\\|^2\ge\varepsilon^2/48.$$
  - Explanation: Translates the analytic midpoint lower bound into a quantitative squared radius deficit for separated families of approximate midpoints.

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Finite-codimensional subspaces** ([source](#eq:average-modulus)): For an infinite-dimensional Banach space $Y$ with norm $N$, write $\operatorname{cof}(Y)$ for the closed finite-codimensional subspaces of $Y$.
- **AUC and averaged AMUC moduli** ([source](#eq:average-modulus)): For $t>0$, define $$\begin{aligned} \overline\delta_N(t) &=\inf_{N(x)=1}\sup_{F\in\operatorname{cof}(Y)} \inf_{y\in F,\,N(y)=1}\bigl(N(x+ty)-1\bigr), \label{eq:auc-modulus}\\ \widehat\delta_N(t) &=\inf_{N(x)=1}\sup_{F\in\operatorname{cof}(Y)} \inf_{y\in F,\,N(y)=1} \left(\frac{N(x+ty)+N(x-ty)}2-1\right). \label{eq:average-modulus} \end{aligned}$$
- **AUC and AMUC norms** ([source](#eq:average-modulus)): The norm is *asymptotically uniformly convex* (AUC) if $\overline\delta_N(t)>0$ for all $t>0$, and *asymptotically midpoint uniformly convex* (AMUC) if $\widehat\delta_N(t)>0$ for all $t>0$.
- **Equivalent norm** ([source](#eq:average-modulus)): A norm $N$ is equivalent to a given norm $\\|\cdot\\|$ if $a\\|x\\|\le N(x)\le b\\|x\\|$ for some $0<a\le b<\infty$ and all $x$.
- **AUC renormability** ([source](#eq:average-modulus)): A space is *AUC renormable* if it admits an equivalent AUC norm.
- **Countably branching diamonds** ([source](#eq:embedding-definition)): Define the *countably branching diamonds* by taking $D_0$ to be a single edge and replacing every edge at each step by countably many internally disjoint two-edge paths.
- **Diamond shortest-path metric** ([source](#eq:embedding-definition)): Each current edge has length one; let $d_k$ be the shortest-path metric on $D_k$.
- **Embedding distortion** ([source](#eq:embedding-definition)): An embedding of $D_k$ into a Banach space has distortion at most $D$ if it is a map $f$ for which some scale $s>0$ satisfies $$\label{eq:embedding-definition} s\,d_k(u,v)\le\\|f(u)-f(v)\\|\le Ds\,d_k(u,v) \qquad(u,v\in D_k).$$
- **Uniform diamond embeddings** ([source](#eq:embedding-definition)): The family embeds uniformly when one finite $D$ works for all $k$.
- **Component tree** ([source](#eq:segment-norm)): For each integer $h\ge1$, let $T_h=\mathbb N^{\le h}$ be the rooted tree of words of length at most $h$, ordered by extension of words.
- **Forest** ([source](#eq:segment-norm)): Keep separate copies of these trees in the forest $$\mathcal F=\coprod_{h\ge1}(\{h\}\times T_h).$$
- **Ancestral order** ([source](#eq:segment-norm)): Write $a\preceq b$ if $a$ is an ancestor of $b$ in the same component.
- **Segment** ([source](#eq:segment-norm)): A *segment* is a nonempty interval $[a,b]=\{v:a\preceq v\preceq b\}$ in an ancestral chain.
- **Segment norm** ([source](#eq:segment-norm)): For a finitely supported real function $u$ on $\mathcal F$, set $u(S)=\sum_{v\in S}u(v)$ and define $$\label{eq:segment-norm} \rho(u)=\sup\left\{\left(\sum_{i=1}^m u(S_i)^2\right)^{1/2}: m\ge1,\ S_1,\ldots,S_m\text{ pairwise disjoint segments}\right\}.$$
- **Predual and dual spaces** ([source](#eq:segment-norm)): Let $J$ be its completion and let $X=J^*$, with the dual norm denoted by $\\|\cdot\\|$.
- **Ancestral head** ([source](#stmt-lem-forest-structure)): Let $H\subset\mathcal F$ be finite and *ancestral*, meaning that it contains every ancestor of each of its vertices.
- **General-forest coordinate completion** ([source](#sec:general-forest)): Define $\rho_T$ by <a href="#eq:segment-norm" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:segment-norm">[eq:segment-norm]</a> with $T$ in place of $\mathcal F$, let $J(T)$ be the completion, and put $$X(T)=\overline{\operatorname{span}}^{\\|\cdot\\|}\{e_v^*:v\in T\} \subset J(T)^*.$$

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Q1 | averaged midpoint modulus \widehat\delta_X(t) | ≥ | $\sqrt{1+t^2/12}-1$ | t>0 |
| Q2 | distortion of embedding of D_k into X | ≥ | $\sqrt{1+k/12}$ | k\ge0 |
| Q3 | averaged norm (\\|x+y\\|+\\|x-y\\|)/2 | ≥ | $\sqrt{\\|x\\|^2+\\|y\\|^2/12}$ | x=P_Hx, y=Q_Hy |
| Q4 | scaled averaged norm (\\|x+ty\\|+\\|x-ty\\|)/2 | ≥ | $\frac{\\|x\\|+st\\|y\\|}{\sqrt{1+12s^2}}$ | s,t>0, x=P_Hx, y=Q_Hy |

| Q6 | squared stretch L_m^2 | ≤ | $L_{m+1}^2-1/12$ | 0\le m<k |
| Q7 | squared distortion D^2 | ≥ | $1+k/12$ | k\ge0 |
| Q8 | maximum-convention modulus m_N(t) | ≤ | $2\widehat\delta_N(t)$ | t>0 |
| Q9 | AUC modulus \overline\delta_N(\alpha c / (2\beta M)) | = | $0$ | 0<\alpha\le\beta<\infty |

Then every equivalent norm satisfying $\\alpha\\|\\cdot\\|\\le N\\le\\beta\\|\\cdot\\|$, with $0<\\alpha\\le\\beta<\\infty$, obeys $$\\label{eq:bounded-tree-scale}\n \\overline\\delta_N\\!\\left(\\frac{\\alpha c}{2\\beta M}\\right)=0.$$ The same conclusion holds for one infinite tree with these properties.",

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **R1** [claim](#stmt-lem-forest-structure) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-forest-structure): Finite-height Hilbert equivalence, duality of square-sum components, Hahn–Banach and coordinate approximation. -> Reflexivity, separability, coordinate density and contractive projections.
- **R2** [claim](#stmt-lem-cones) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-cones): Group disjoint tests by gates and use square-sum duality. -> Exact tail decomposition and gate-coordinate contraction.
- **R3** [claim](#stmt-lem-modulus-conventions) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-modulus-conventions): Even convex radial functions and intersection with a norming-functional kernel. -> Modulus comparisons and symmetric monotonicity.
- **R4** [claim](#stmt-lem-bounded-tree) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-bounded-tree): Finite-dimensional quotient approximation and multiplicative-growth contradiction. -> Vanishing one-sided modulus for every equivalent norm.
- **R5** [claim](#stmt-lem-paired) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-paired): Gate correction, simultaneous suffix cuts, cancellation in the endpoint average and optimization. -> Paired head–tail inequalities.
- **R6** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-paired-2): Finite ancestral approximation of arbitrary centers and limiting the error. -> Explicit averaged AMUC lower bound.
- **R7** [claim](#stmt-lem-lens) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-lens): Finite-dimensional compactness, half-differences and the paired inequality. -> Squared deficit for infinitely many separated midpoints.
- **R8** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-lens-2): Normalize the embedding and telescope squared stretch losses through refinements. -> Diamond distortion lower bound.
- **R9** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#sec:no-auc): Apply the bounded-tree obstruction to norm-one root paths and weakly null child coordinate increments. -> No equivalent AUC norm on the specified reflexive forest dual.
- **R10** [claim](#eq:general-forest-modulus) / [proof](#sec:general-forest): Repeat finite-support gate corrections and ancestral approximation in the coordinate completion. -> The same midpoint lower bound without identifying the completion with the full dual.

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **I-finite-segment-norm**: The squared norm of a finitely supported vector is the supremum of sums of squared segment sums over pairwise disjoint segment families.
- **I-paired**: The head-tail average has the stated square-root lower bound.
- **I-lens**: An infinite separated midpoint family forces the stated squared-radius deficit.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **A-finite-diamonds**: Apply the countably branching distortion bound to arbitrary finite diamond samples. -> The compactness argument selects a pair from an infinite separated family; a finite sample does not supply that hypothesis.
- **A-full-dual**: Identify the general-forest coordinate completion with its full dual. -> The source expressly limits the general-forest construction to the coordinate completion.
- **A-renorm-constants**: Reuse the same numerical distortion constants after an arbitrary equivalent renorming. -> Norm equivalence rescales distortion constants.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. Finite restriction of the finitely supported segment-norm definition at eq:segment-norm, not a certification of the infinite-dimensional theorem. Every finite input forest embeds as a finite ancestral subset of the source forest, assigning zero to all other vertices; segment intersections remain intervals, so this finite computation is exact. No dual-norm computation or engineering application is claimed.

```python
# INPUTS: JSON object with cases, each containing parent and rational-string weights arrays.
# PRECONDITIONS: at most 200 cases; at most 7 vertices each; parent[i] is -1 or 0 <= parent[i] < i; rational weights.
# OUTPUTS: JSON array of exact rational strings equal to rho(u)^2 for each finite rooted forest.
# TIME: O(C * (n^2 * 2^n + n^3)), for C cases with at most n vertices.
# SPACE: O(n^2 + 2^n) per case, excluding input/output; rational bit growth depends on input sizes.
# ARITHMETIC: python-flint fmpq exact rational operations, no floating point.
# FAILURE: malformed input or violated finite-size/predecessor conditions raises an exception.
import sys, json
from functools import lru_cache
from flint import fmpq

def squared_norm(case):
    p, raw = case['parent'], case['weights']
    n = len(p)
    assert n <= 7 and len(raw) == n
    assert all(type(q) is int and -1 <= q < i for i,q in enumerate(p))
    a = [fmpq(q) for q in raw]
    segments = []
    for end in range(n):
        node, mask, total = end, 0, fmpq(0)
        while node >= 0:
            mask |= 1 << node
            total += a[node]
            segments.append((mask, total * total))
            node = p[node]
    @lru_cache(None)
    def pack(remaining):
        best = fmpq(0)
        for mask, weight in segments:
            if mask & remaining == mask:
                best = max(best, weight + pack(remaining ^ mask))
        return best
    return str(pack((1 << n) - 1))
payload = json.loads(sys.stdin.read())
assert len(payload['cases']) <= 200
print(json.dumps([squared_norm(c) for c in payload['cases']]))
```

## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **L1** (hypothesis): The development is restricted to real spaces and scalars. Blocked use: ['Transfer to complex Banach spaces without a separate proof.']
- **L2** (counterexample_scope): The example separates AMUC of its given norm from AUC renormability; it does not identify these properties. Blocked use: ['Inferring AUC renormability from AMUC or from diamond nonembedding alone.']
- **L3** (hypothesis): The numerical estimates refer to the specified dual norm; equivalent renorming preserves the uniform embedding obstruction but need not preserve its constants. Blocked use: ['Reusing the numerical constants unchanged for an arbitrary equivalent norm.']
- **L4** (hypothesis): The quantitative midpoint modulus uses the average convention. Positivity equivalence with the maximum convention does not make their numerical values identical. Blocked use: ['Treating the historical maximum modulus as equal to the average modulus.']
- **L5** (hypothesis): Contractive head and tail projections and the paired estimate require a finite ancestral head. Blocked use: ['Applying the paired estimate to an arbitrary coordinate split without ancestor closure.']
- **L6** (hypothesis): The paired estimate assumes that the center is supported on the head and the displacement on its complement. Arbitrary centers require approximation before the modulus bound follows. Blocked use: ['Applying the split estimate directly to overlapping head and tail supports.']
- **L7** (counterexample_scope): Finite height of each component does not mean a finite forest or a uniform Hilbert equivalence across heights. Blocked use: ['Replacing the example by a finite tree and asserting the same renorming obstruction.', 'Claiming a height-independent Hilbert equivalence from the component estimate.']
- **L8** (hypothesis): The bounded-tree obstruction needs arbitrarily high trees with uniform bounds and weakly null child increments; one bounded finite tree is insufficient. Blocked use: ['Deducing failure of AUC renormability from finitely many bounded paths or sampled children.']
- **L9** (formal_scope): The general-forest estimate concerns the coordinate completion in the polar norm, not an asserted identification with the full dual. Blocked use: ['Promoting the general-forest modulus estimate to all of the full dual without coordinate density.']
- **L10** (hypothesis): The general-forest extension assumes a countably infinite rooted forest with finitely many ancestors at every vertex. Blocked use: ['Transferring the extension to arbitrary ordered sets or forests lacking finite ancestral closures.']
- **L11** (nonconstructive): The lens proof uses an infinite separated family and compactness of finite-dimensional projections. A finite sampled family does not establish this premise. Blocked use: ['Certifying the lens or diamond obstruction from finite midpoint samples alone.']
- **L12** (hypothesis): The diamond theorem uses countably branching diamonds and the full bi-Lipschitz convention at every vertex pair. Blocked use: ['Applying the same theorem unchanged to finite binary diamonds or only selected vertex-pair constraints.']
- **L13** (edge_case): Stretch suprema in the diamond argument need not be attained. Selecting a maximizing edge requires an additional argument. Blocked use: ['Implementing the proof by assuming the existence of a maximizing edge at every level.']
- **L14** (edge_case): The depth-zero distortion assertion gives only the baseline noncontraction bound. Blocked use: ['Claiming a strictly greater distortion bound at depth zero.']
- **L15** (counterexample_scope): The counterexample blocks a diamond converse for general reflexive spaces; it does not negate converses that impose unconditional asymptotic structure. Blocked use: ['Removing the unconditional-structure hypothesis from previously cited positive converse results.']
- **L16** (unknown): A concrete software-catalogue application contract is ABSENT from the supplied context; the mathematical obstruction alone does not certify an engineering use. Blocked use: ['Claiming a software guarantee without an explicit contract connecting its objects and hypotheses to the paper.']

<a id="pm-formal"></a>
### Formal statement (Lean 4)

Corpus status: formalized in the source corpus (toolchain `leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1`, Mathlib `d13f23b723b8a846827a245b89c10fc7d3f11612`). Not re-checked here; the statements give the exact quantifiers and hypotheses, not a proof.

Scope note of family 331 (`lean/docs/331.md`, verbatim):

> The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):
>
> - [Asymptotic midpoint uniform convexity and unbounded diamond distortion in a reflexive tree space](../../preprints/Asymptotic-midpoint-uniform-convexity-and-unbounded-diamond-distortion-in-a-reflexive-tree-space-September-27-2026/manuscript.pdf)
> - [Midpoint lenses in segment spaces](../../preprints/Midpoint-lenses-in-segment-spaces-September-27-2026/manuscript.pdf)
> - [Distortion of countably branching diamonds from midpoint and tree energies](../../preprints/Diamond-distortion-from-midpoint-and-tree-energies-September-27-2026/manuscript.pdf)
> - [Exact asymptotic moduli in a Daugavet subspace of $L_1$](../../preprints/Exact-asymptotic-moduli-in-a-Daugavet-subspace-of-L1-September-27-2026/manuscript.pdf)
> - [Midpoint convexity from bounded tree potentials and path costs](../../preprints/Midpoint-convexity-from-bounded-tree-potentials-and-path-costs-September-27-2026/manuscript.pdf)
> - [Independent products in real $L_1$: asymptotic midpoint convexity without AUC renormings](../../preprints/Independent-products-in-real-L1-asymptotic-midpoint-convexity-without-AUC-renormings-September-27-2026/manuscript.pdf)
> - [Midpoint convexity from two recursive potentials](../../preprints/Midpoint-convexity-from-two-recursive-potentials-September-27-2026/manuscript.pdf)
>
> ## Scope
>
> The paper constructs a Banach space whose midpoint geometry is asymptotically uniformly convex even though no equivalent norm is asymptotically uniformly convex in the usual one-sided sense. The formalization proves that the full dual of the specified segment-norm completion is infinite-dimensional, separable, and reflexive; its averaged midpoint modulus is at least $\sqrt{1+t^2/12}-1$ for every $t>0$, while every equivalent norm fails asymptotic uniform convexity. It also proves that an embedding of a depth-$k$ countably branching diamond has distortion at least $\sqrt{1+k/12}$.
>
> The general-forest and word-forest results use the closed span of the coordinate functionals. Their conclusions retain the finite-ancestor and equivalent-norm hypotheses, including the scale $\alpha/(2\beta)$ when $0<\alpha\le\beta$ and the new norm lies between $\alpha$ and $\beta$ times the original norm; they do not identify that span with the full dual or assert reflexivity for every forest.
>
> The paper controls midpoint lenses in Banach spaces defined by tree-segment norms. The formalization proves the following estimate in both the full dual of the finite-height forest space and the coordinate predual of the infinite-tree space. If $x$ is supported on a finite ancestral set $H$, $R\ge0$, and $\|x+y\|,\|x-y\|\le R$, then the part of $y$ outside $H$ has norm at most $2\sqrt{R^2-\|x\|^2}$.
>
> The formalization also covers selected consequences for averaged midpoint moduli and separated families of vectors, together with reflexivity and renorming obstructions. This scope concerns the segment-space results; the companion results on diamond energies remain separate.
>
> The paper studies embeddings of countably branching diamond graphs into Banach spaces built from tree segments. The formalization proves that every embedding of the depth-$k$ diamond with distortion $D$, at any positive scale, satisfies $1+k/4\le D^2$ in either of two spaces: the full dual of the completed finite-height forest space and the closed coordinate span in the dual of the infinite-tree space. The bound applies to all pairs of vertices and is unconditional.
>
> The formalization also contains selected graph deductions under explicit companion assumptions. Twenty-two of those companion inputs are assumed in these deductions; the diamond distortion bound above does not require them.
>
> The paper exhibits a real $L_1$ subspace with positive averaged midpoint convexity but no equivalent asymptotically uniformly convex norm. For every closed infinite-dimensional subspace whose unit ball is precompact in measure and which has the Daugavet property, the formalization computes the moduli at every unit vector: the averaged midpoint modulus is $\max(t/2,t-1)$ and the usual one-sided asymptotic modulus is $\max(0,t-2)$ for every $t>0$. The same formulas hold after taking the infimum over unit vectors, and every equivalent norm fails asymptotic uniform convexity.
>
> The formalization also constructs such a subspace on a countable product of unit intervals and proves that it has the stated measure-precompactness and Daugavet properties, so the modulus formulas apply to an actual example.
>
> The paper constructs Banach spaces from bounded tree potentials and path costs to separate averaged midpoint convexity from asymptotic uniform convexity. For the specified tree-potential completions, the formalization proves completeness, separability, infinite dimension, and an averaged midpoint modulus of at least $\sqrt{1+t^2/4}-1$ for $0<t<1$. No space linearly isomorphic to one of these completions is asymptotically uniformly convex.
>
> The formalization also covers selected path-cost duality and comparison results, clipping and energy estimates, and further renorming obstructions. These retain their stated support, head, and tail hypotheses; the remaining auxiliary assertions of the paper are outside this scope.
>
> The paper forms a real $L_1$ space from the closed span of products of independent multipliers along tree paths. For a positive, nonconstant multiplier of mean one and finite second moment, the formalization proves that this space is infinite-dimensional and has no equivalent asymptotically uniformly convex norm.
>
> For every $t>0$, the averaged midpoint modulus nevertheless has explicit positive lower bounds. If $G$ is a standard real Gaussian, the exponential-multiplier bound is $\frac{7t}{480}\mathbb E[(|G|-20/t)_+]$, where $u_+=\max(u,0)$. For squared-Gaussian multipliers, the bounds are $\frac18\Pr(|G|\ge4\sqrt2(2+\sqrt2)/t)$ and $\frac14\Pr(|G|\ge8\sqrt6/t)$.
>
> The paper constructs three Banach spaces from recursive tree potentials: a root-sum space, a zero-root space, and a joined space. The formalization proves that all three are infinite-dimensional and have no equivalent asymptotically uniformly convex norm. The root-sum and joined spaces have averaged midpoint modulus at least $t^3/128$ for $0<t<1$, and the root-sum modulus is positive for every $t>0$. The zero-root estimate retains its finite ancestral support and tail hypotheses, and the zero-root and joined spaces are reflexive.
>
> The formalization also covers a separated-midpoint result for the joined space and a variable-exponent model with its recursive norm, duality, projections, renorming obstruction, and sixth- and third-order stability estimates. The separation, sequence, support, and head assumptions in these results are essential parts of their statements.

```lean
import Mathlib

namespace OAI

noncomputable section

open scoped BigOperators
open Finset

namespace ForestSpace

attribute [local instance] Classical.propDecidable

section ModulusDefinitions

variable (E : Type) [NormedAddCommGroup E] [NormedSpace ℝ E]

/-- The closed finite-codimensional subspaces. -/
def Cofinite : Type :=
  {F : Submodule ℝ E // IsClosed (F : Set E) ∧ FiniteDimensional ℝ (E ⧸ F)}

/-- The inner one-sided excess values and their infimum. -/
def aucValues (N : E → ℝ) (t : ℝ) (x : E) (F : Cofinite E) : Set ℝ :=
  {a | ∃ y : E, y ∈ F.val ∧ N y = 1 ∧ a = N (x + t • y) - 1}

noncomputable def aucInner (N : E → ℝ) (t : ℝ) (x : E) (F : Cofinite E) : ℝ :=
  sInf (aucValues E N t x F)

noncomputable def aucCenter (N : E → ℝ) (t : ℝ) (x : E) : ℝ :=
  sSup (Set.range (aucInner E N t x))

/-- The one-sided asymptotic uniform convexity modulus, with the order
of infimum, supremum and infimum. -/
noncomputable def aucModulus (N : E → ℝ) (t : ℝ) : ℝ :=
  sInf {a | ∃ x : E, N x = 1 ∧ a = aucCenter E N t x}

/-- The inner averaged signed excess; it is not replaced by a maximum. -/
def averageValues (N : E → ℝ) (t : ℝ) (x : E) (F : Cofinite E) : Set ℝ :=
  {a | ∃ y : E, y ∈ F.val ∧ N y = 1 ∧
    a = (N (x+t•y) + N (x-t•y))/((2 : ℕ) : ℝ) - 1}

noncomputable def averageInner (N : E → ℝ) (t : ℝ) (x : E) (F : Cofinite E) : ℝ :=
  sInf (averageValues E N t x F)

noncomputable def averageCenter (N : E → ℝ) (t : ℝ) (x : E) : ℝ :=
  sSup (Set.range (averageInner E N t x))

/-- The averaged asymptotic midpoint modulus. -/
noncomputable def averageModulus (N : E → ℝ) (t : ℝ) : ℝ :=
  sInf {a | ∃ x : E, N x = 1 ∧ a = averageCenter E N t x}

end ModulusDefinitions

/-- An equivalent norm is kept as its exact real seminorm, with explicit positive
comparison constants. The lower inequality makes it a norm. -/
structure EquivalentNorm (E : Type) [NormedAddCommGroup E] [NormedSpace ℝ E]
    (α β : ℝ) where
  N : Seminorm ℝ E
  alpha_pos : 0 < α
  alpha_le_beta : α ≤ β
  lower : ∀ x : E, α*‖x‖ ≤ N x
  upper : ∀ x : E, N x ≤ β*‖x‖

/-- Canonical (not merely abstract) reflexivity. -/
def Reflexive (E : Type) [NormedAddCommGroup E] [NormedSpace ℝ E] : Prop :=
  Function.Surjective (NormedSpace.inclusionInDoubleDual ℝ E)

/-- AUC requires positivity at every strictly positive parameter. -/
def IsAUC {E : Type} [NormedAddCommGroup E] [NormedSpace ℝ E] (N : E → ℝ) : Prop :=
  ∀ t : ℝ, 0 < t → 0 < aucModulus E N t

/-- A finite, order-convex chain. The empty chain is a harmless zero test. -/
def IsSegment {V : Type} [PartialOrder V] (S : Finset V) : Prop :=
  (∀ a ∈ S, ∀ b ∈ S, a ≤ b ∨ b ≤ a) ∧
  (∀ a ∈ S, ∀ b ∈ S, ∀ c, a ≤ c → c ≤ b → c ∈ S)

/-- The sum of coordinates over a segment. -/
def segSum {V : Type} (u : V → ℝ) (S : Finset V) : ℝ := ∑ v ∈ S, u v

/-- Squared bound on every disjoint family of segment tests. -/
def TestBound {V : Type} [PartialOrder V] (u : V → ℝ) (C : ℝ) : Prop :=
  ∀ (ι : Type) (_ : Fintype ι) (S : ι → Finset V),
    (∀ i, IsSegment (S i)) → Pairwise (fun i j => Disjoint (S i) (S j)) →
    ∑ i, segSum u (S i) ^ 2 ≤ C

section SegmentNorm

variable {V : Type} [PartialOrder V]

/-- All squared disjoint-segment tests. Quantifying over finite index types
is equivalent to families S₁,...,Sₘ; a zero-sized family
adds only zero to the set of test values. -/
def testValues (u : V → ℝ) : Set ℝ :=
  {r | ∃ (ι : Type) (_ : Fintype ι) (S : ι → Finset V),
    (∀ i, IsSegment (S i)) ∧ Pairwise (fun i j => Disjoint (S i) (S j)) ∧
    r = ∑ i, segSum u (S i) ^ 2}

noncomputable def squareSup (u : V → ℝ) : ℝ := sSup (testValues u)

/-- Exactly the disjoint-segment norm, written as the square root of its
squared testing supremum. -/
noncomputable def rho (u : V → ℝ) : ℝ := Real.sqrt (squareSup u)

noncomputable def l1size (u : V →₀ ℝ) : ℝ := ∑ v ∈ u.support, |u v|

end SegmentNorm

/-- The disjoint-segment seminorm on finite coordinate vectors. -/
def segmentSeminorm (V : Type) [PartialOrder V] : Seminorm ℝ (V →₀ ℝ) := by
  have segSum_zero [PartialOrder V] (S : Finset V) : segSum (0 : V → ℝ) S = 0 := by
    simp [segSum]
  have segSum_add [PartialOrder V] (u v : V → ℝ) (S : Finset V) :
      segSum (u + v) S = segSum u S + segSum v S := by
    simp [segSum, Finset.sum_add_distrib]
  have segSum_smul [PartialOrder V] (a : ℝ) (u : V → ℝ) (S : Finset V) :
      segSum (a • u) S = a * segSum u S := by
    simp [segSum, Finset.mul_sum]
  have TestBound.nonneg {u : V → ℝ} {C : ℝ} (h : TestBound u C) : 0 ≤ C := by
    simpa using h PEmpty inferInstance PEmpty.elim (by simp) (by simp [Pairwise])
  have TestBound.zero : TestBound (0 : V → ℝ) 0 := by
    intro ι inst S hS hd
    simp [segSum]
  have TestBound.smul {u : V → ℝ} {C : ℝ} (h : TestBound u C) (a : ℝ) :
      TestBound (a • u) (a ^ 2 * C) := by
    intro ι inst S hS hd
    have hh := mul_le_mul_of_nonneg_left (h ι inst S hS hd) (sq_nonneg a)
    simpa only [segSum_smul, mul_pow, Finset.mul_sum] using hh
  have TestBound.add {u v : V → ℝ} {A B : ℝ}
      (hu : TestBound u (A ^ 2)) (hv : TestBound v (B ^ 2))
      (hA : 0 ≤ A) (hB : 0 ≤ B) : TestBound (u + v) ((A + B) ^ 2) := by
    intro ι inst S hS hd
    have h₁ := hu ι inst S hS hd
    have h₂ := hv ι inst S hS hd
    have hc := Real.sum_mul_le_sqrt_mul_sqrt (univ : Finset ι)
      (fun i => segSum u (S i)) (fun i => segSum v (S i))
    have hs₁ : Real.sqrt (∑ i, segSum u (S i) ^ 2) ≤ A :=
      (Real.sqrt_le_iff).2 ⟨hA, h₁⟩
    have hs₂ : Real.sqrt (∑ i, segSum v (S i) ^ 2) ≤ B :=
      (Real.sqrt_le_iff).2 ⟨hB, h₂⟩
    have hc' : ∑ i, segSum u (S i) * segSum v (S i) ≤ A * B :=
      hc.trans (mul_le_mul hs₁ hs₂ (Real.sqrt_nonneg _) hA)
    have he : (∑ i, segSum (u + v) (S i) ^ 2) =
        (∑ i, segSum u (S i) ^ 2) + 2 * (∑ i, segSum u (S i) * segSum v (S i)) +
        (∑ i, segSum v (S i) ^ 2) := by
      simp only [segSum_add, add_sq, Finset.sum_add_distrib, Finset.mul_sum]
      congr 1
      congr 1
      apply Finset.sum_congr rfl
      intro i hi
      ring
    rw [he]
    nlinarith
  have zero_mem_testValues (u : V → ℝ) : 0 ∈ testValues u := by
    refine ⟨PEmpty, inferInstance, PEmpty.elim, by simp, by simp [Pairwise], ?_⟩
    simp [segSum]
  have TestBound.bddAbove {u : V → ℝ} {C : ℝ} (h : TestBound u C) :
      BddAbove (testValues u) := by
    refine ⟨C, ?_⟩
    rintro r ⟨ι, inst, S, hs, hd, rfl⟩
    exact h ι inst S hs hd
  have TestBound.squareSup_le {u : V → ℝ} {C : ℝ} (h : TestBound u C) :
      squareSup u ≤ C := by
    apply csSup_le ⟨0, zero_mem_testValues u⟩
    rintro r ⟨ι, inst, S, hs, hd, rfl⟩
    exact h ι inst S hs hd
  have testBound_squareSup {u : V → ℝ} (hu : ∃ C, TestBound u C) :
      TestBound u (squareSup u) := by
    intro ι inst S hs hd
    exact le_csSup (TestBound.bddAbove hu.choose_spec) ⟨ι, inst, S, hs, hd, rfl⟩
  have squareSup_nonneg {u : V → ℝ} (hu : ∃ C, TestBound u C) : 0 ≤ squareSup u :=
    TestBound.nonneg (testBound_squareSup hu)
  have rho_nonneg (u : V → ℝ) : 0 ≤ rho u := Real.sqrt_nonneg _
  have rho_sq {u : V → ℝ} (hu : ∃ C, TestBound u C) : rho u ^ 2 = squareSup u :=
    Real.sq_sqrt (squareSup_nonneg hu)
  have testBound_rho_sq {u : V → ℝ} (hu : ∃ C, TestBound u C) : TestBound u (rho u ^ 2) := by
    rw [rho_sq hu]
    exact testBound_squareSup hu
  have rho_le_of_testBound {u : V → ℝ} {C : ℝ} (hC : 0 ≤ C)
      (hu : TestBound u (C ^ 2)) : rho u ≤ C :=
    Real.sqrt_le_iff.mpr ⟨hC, TestBound.squareSup_le hu⟩
  have rho_add_le {u v : V → ℝ} (hu : ∃ C, TestBound u C) (hv : ∃ C, TestBound v C) :
      rho (u + v) ≤ rho u + rho v :=
    rho_le_of_testBound (add_nonneg (rho_nonneg u) (rho_nonneg v))
      (TestBound.add (testBound_rho_sq hu) (testBound_rho_sq hv) (rho_nonneg u) (rho_nonneg v))
  have rho_smul_le {u : V → ℝ} (hu : ∃ C, TestBound u C) (a : ℝ) :
      rho (a • u) ≤ |a| * rho u := by
    apply rho_le_of_testBound (mul_nonneg (abs_nonneg a) (rho_nonneg u))
    have hh := TestBound.smul (testBound_rho_sq hu) a
    simpa only [mul_pow, sq_abs] using hh
  have rho_zero : rho (0 : V → ℝ) = 0 := by
    apply le_antisymm _ (rho_nonneg _)
    apply rho_le_of_testBound (C := 0) (by norm_num)
    simpa using (TestBound.zero : TestBound (0 : V → ℝ) 0)
  have l1size_nonneg [PartialOrder V] (u : V →₀ ℝ) : 0 ≤ l1size u :=
    sum_nonneg (fun vertex _ => abs_nonneg (u vertex))
  have finset_abs_sum_le_l1size [PartialOrder V] (u : V →₀ ℝ) (S : Finset V) :
      ∑ v ∈ S, |u v| ≤ l1size u := by
    have he : (∑ v ∈ S ∪ u.support, |u v|) = l1size u := by
      symm
      apply sum_subset subset_union_right
      intro v hv hn
      rw [Finsupp.notMem_support_iff.mp hn, abs_zero]
    rw [← he]
    exact sum_le_sum_of_subset_of_nonneg subset_union_left (fun v hv hn => abs_nonneg _)
  have testBound_l1size (u : V →₀ ℝ) : TestBound u (l1size u ^ 2) := by
    intro ι inst S hS hd
    have habs : (∑ i, |segSum u (S i)|) ≤ l1size u := by
      calc
        (∑ i, |segSum u (S i)|) ≤ ∑ i, ∑ v ∈ S i, |u v| :=
          sum_le_sum fun i hi => abs_sum_le_sum_abs _ _
        _ = ∑ v ∈ univ.biUnion S, |u v| :=
          (sum_biUnion (by intro i hi j hj hij; exact hd hij)).symm
        _ ≤ l1size u := finset_abs_sum_le_l1size u _
    have hsq := sum_sq_le_sq_sum_of_nonneg
      (s := (univ : Finset ι)) (f := fun i => |segSum u (S i)|) (fun i hi => abs_nonneg _)
    simp only [sq_abs] at hsq
    have hnonneg : 0 ≤ ∑ i, |segSum u (S i)| := sum_nonneg (fun i hi => abs_nonneg _)
    nlinarith [l1size_nonneg u]
  have finsupp_has_testBound (u : V →₀ ℝ) : ∃ C, TestBound u C := ⟨_, testBound_l1size u⟩
  exact Seminorm.ofSMulLE (fun u : V →₀ ℝ => rho u) rho_zero
    (fun u v => rho_add_le (finsupp_has_testBound u) (finsupp_has_testBound v))
    (fun a u => by simpa only [Real.norm_eq_abs, Finsupp.coe_smul] using rho_smul_le (finsupp_has_testBound u) a)

/-- Finite coordinate vectors with the segment norm (a type synonym prevents
clashes with any other norm on finitely supported functions). -/
def TestVector (V : Type) := V →₀ ℝ

namespace TestVector

variable {V : Type}

instance : AddCommGroup (TestVector V) := inferInstanceAs (AddCommGroup (V →₀ ℝ))

instance : Module ℝ (TestVector V) := inferInstanceAs (Module ℝ (V →₀ ℝ))

def toFinsupp (u : TestVector V) : V →₀ ℝ := u

instance : CoeFun (TestVector V) (fun _ => V → ℝ) := ⟨fun u => u.toFinsupp⟩

variable [PartialOrder V]

instance : Norm (TestVector V) := ⟨fun u => rho u⟩

instance : NormedAddCommGroup (TestVector V) := by
  have isSegment_singleton (v : V) : IsSegment ({v} : Finset V) := by
    constructor
    · intro a ha b hb
      simp only [mem_singleton] at ha hb
      subst a
      subst b
      exact Or.inl le_rfl
    · intro a ha b hb c hac hcb
      simp only [mem_singleton] at ha hb ⊢
      subst a
      subst b
      exact le_antisymm hcb hac
  have TestBound.nonneg {u : V → ℝ} {C : ℝ} (h : TestBound u C) : 0 ≤ C := by
    simpa using h PEmpty inferInstance PEmpty.elim (by simp) (by simp [Pairwise])
  have TestBound.single {u : V → ℝ} {C : ℝ} (h : TestBound u C)
      {S : Finset V} (hS : IsSegment S) : segSum u S ^ 2 ≤ C := by
    simpa using h Unit inferInstance (fun _ => S) (fun _ => hS)
      (by simp [Pairwise])
  have TestBound.bddAbove {u : V → ℝ} {C : ℝ} (h : TestBound u C) :
      BddAbove (testValues u) := by
    refine ⟨C, ?_⟩
    rintro r ⟨ι, inst, S, hs, hd, rfl⟩
    exact h ι inst S hs hd
  have testBound_squareSup {u : V → ℝ} (hu : ∃ C, TestBound u C) :
      TestBound u (squareSup u) := by
    intro ι inst S hs hd
    exact le_csSup (TestBound.bddAbove hu.choose_spec) ⟨ι, inst, S, hs, hd, rfl⟩
  have squareSup_nonneg {u : V → ℝ} (hu : ∃ C, TestBound u C) : 0 ≤ squareSup u :=
    TestBound.nonneg (testBound_squareSup hu)
  have rho_nonneg (u : V → ℝ) : 0 ≤ rho u := Real.sqrt_nonneg _
  have rho_sq {u : V → ℝ} (hu : ∃ C, TestBound u C) : rho u ^ 2 = squareSup u :=
    Real.sq_sqrt (squareSup_nonneg hu)
  have testBound_rho_sq {u : V → ℝ} (hu : ∃ C, TestBound u C) : TestBound u (rho u ^ 2) := by
    rw [rho_sq hu]
    exact testBound_squareSup hu
  have coordinate_le_rho {u : V → ℝ} (hu : ∃ C, TestBound u C) (v : V) :
      |u v| ≤ rho u := by
    have hh := TestBound.single (testBound_rho_sq hu) (isSegment_singleton v)
    simp only [segSum, sum_singleton] at hh
    exact (sq_le_sq₀ (abs_nonneg _) (rho_nonneg _)).mp (by simpa only [sq_abs] using hh)
  have l1size_nonneg [PartialOrder V] (u : V →₀ ℝ) : 0 ≤ l1size u :=
    sum_nonneg (fun vertex _ => abs_nonneg (u vertex))
  have finset_abs_sum_le_l1size [PartialOrder V] (u : V →₀ ℝ) (S : Finset V) :
      ∑ v ∈ S, |u v| ≤ l1size u := by
    have he : (∑ v ∈ S ∪ u.support, |u v|) = l1size u := by
      symm
      apply sum_subset subset_union_right
      intro v hv hn
      rw [Finsupp.notMem_support_iff.mp hn, abs_zero]
    rw [← he]
    exact sum_le_sum_of_subset_of_nonneg subset_union_left (fun v hv hn => abs_nonneg _)
  have testBound_l1size (u : V →₀ ℝ) : TestBound u (l1size u ^ 2) := by
    intro ι inst S hS hd
    have habs : (∑ i, |segSum u (S i)|) ≤ l1size u := by
      calc
        (∑ i, |segSum u (S i)|) ≤ ∑ i, ∑ v ∈ S i, |u v| :=
          sum_le_sum fun i hi => abs_sum_le_sum_abs _ _
        _ = ∑ v ∈ univ.biUnion S, |u v| :=
          (sum_biUnion (by intro i hi j hj hij; exact hd hij)).symm
        _ ≤ l1size u := finset_abs_sum_le_l1size u _
    have hsq := sum_sq_le_sq_sum_of_nonneg
      (s := (univ : Finset ι)) (f := fun i => |segSum u (S i)|) (fun i hi => abs_nonneg _)
    simp only [sq_abs] at hsq
    have hnonneg : 0 ≤ ∑ i, |segSum u (S i)| := sum_nonneg (fun i hi => abs_nonneg _)
    nlinarith [l1size_nonneg u]
  have finsupp_has_testBound (u : V →₀ ℝ) : ∃ C, TestBound u C := ⟨_, testBound_l1size u⟩
  exact NormedAddCommGroup.ofCore (𝕜 := ℝ) {
    norm_nonneg u := apply_nonneg (segmentSeminorm V) u.toFinsupp
    norm_triangle u v := map_add_le_add (segmentSeminorm V) u.toFinsupp v.toFinsupp
    norm_smul a u := map_smul_eq_mul (segmentSeminorm V) a u.toFinsupp
    norm_eq_zero_iff u := ⟨by
      intro h
      apply Finsupp.ext
      intro v
      have hh := coordinate_le_rho (finsupp_has_testBound u.toFinsupp) v
      change rho u.toFinsupp = 0 at h
      rw [h] at hh
      exact abs_nonpos_iff.mp hh, by
      rintro rfl
      exact map_zero (segmentSeminorm V)⟩ }

instance : NormedSpace ℝ (TestVector V) where
  norm_smul_le a u := le_of_eq (map_smul_eq_mul (segmentSeminorm V) a u.toFinsupp)

end TestVector

/-- J(T), completion of the finite-support disjoint-segment norm. -/
abbrev J (V : Type) [PartialOrder V] := UniformSpace.Completion (TestVector V)

/-- The full continuous dual, used for the finite-height forest example. -/
abbrev FullDual (V : Type) [PartialOrder V] := StrongDual ℝ (J V)

section CoordinateCompletion

variable {W : no_index Type} [PartialOrder W]

instance fullDualAddCommGroup : AddCommGroup (FullDual W) :=
  inferInstanceAs (AddCommGroup (StrongDual ℝ (J W)))

end CoordinateCompletion

/-- Finite words, ordered by extension, including the empty root. -/
structure Word where
  letters : List ℕ
  deriving DecidableEq

namespace Word

instance : PartialOrder Word where
  le a b := a.letters <+: b.letters
  le_refl _ := List.prefix_rfl
  le_trans _ _ _ := List.IsPrefix.trans
  le_antisymm a b hab hba := by
    have h := hab.eq_of_length (Nat.le_antisymm hab.length_le hba.length_le)
    cases a; cases b; cases h; rfl

end Word

/-- A disjoint forest: one copy of `Nat^{≤h}` for every `h≥1`. -/
structure Vertex where
  height : ℕ
  positive : 1 ≤ height
  word : Word
  bounded : word.letters.length ≤ height
  deriving DecidableEq

namespace Vertex

instance : PartialOrder Vertex where
  le a b := a.height = b.height ∧ a.word ≤ b.word
  le_refl _ := ⟨rfl,le_rfl⟩
  le_trans a b c hab hbc := ⟨hab.1.trans hbc.1,hab.2.trans hbc.2⟩
  le_antisymm a b hab hba := by
    obtain ⟨hh, hw⟩ := hab
    have he := le_antisymm hw hba.2
    cases a; cases b; cases hh; cases he; rfl

end Vertex

namespace Diamond

/-- An oriented edge enumeration; only the associated undirected unit-edge
simple graph is used for distances. -/
structure Network where
  V : Type
  E : Type
  left : E → V
  right : E → V
  distinct : ∀ e, left e ≠ right e

namespace Network

variable (A : Network)

def graph : SimpleGraph A.V where
  Adj u v := ∃ e : A.E, (A.left e = u ∧ A.right e = v) ∨
    (A.right e = u ∧ A.left e = v)
  symm := ⟨by
    rintro u v ⟨e,h | h⟩
    · exact ⟨e,Or.inr ⟨h.2,h.1⟩⟩
    · exact ⟨e,Or.inl ⟨h.2,h.1⟩⟩⟩
  loopless := ⟨by
    rintro u ⟨e,h | h⟩
    · exact A.distinct e (h.1.trans h.2.symm)
    · exact A.distinct e (h.2.trans h.1.symm)⟩

/-- Every edge is replaced by countably many internally disjoint two-edge
paths. All old vertices remain, and each pair (edge,index) is a new midpoint. -/
@[reducible] def refine : Network where
  V := Sum A.V (A.E × ℕ)
  E := A.E × ℕ × Bool
  left e := if e.2.2 then Sum.inr (e.1,e.2.1) else Sum.inl (A.left e.1)
  right e := if e.2.2 then Sum.inl (A.right e.1) else Sum.inr (e.1,e.2.1)
  distinct := by rintro ⟨e,j,b⟩; cases b <;> simp

/-- Iterating the very same edge replacement, starting from an arbitrary
network. This recursion groups the first replacement before the remaining ones. -/
@[reducible] def iterate : Network → ℕ → Network
  | A,0 => A
  | A,n+1 => iterate A.refine n

end Network

/-- D₀ is precisely one edge. -/
@[reducible] def base : Network where
  V := Bool
  E := Unit
  left _ := false
  right _ := true
  distinct _ := by decide

/-- Countably branching diamonds, all current edges of length one. -/
@[reducible] def diamond (k : ℕ) := base.iterate k

/-- The shortest-path distance, via Mathlib's actual graph distance. -/
def distance (k : ℕ) (u v : (diamond k).V) : ℝ := ((diamond k).graph.dist u v : ℝ)
variable {E : Type} [NormedAddCommGroup E] [NormedSpace ℝ E]

/-- The distortion convention, including its arbitrary positive scale. -/
def EmbeddingBound (k : ℕ) (f : (diamond k).V → E) (C : ℝ) : Prop :=
  ∃ s : ℝ, 0 < s ∧ ∀ u v,
    s*distance k u v ≤ ‖f u-f v‖ ∧ ‖f u-f v‖ ≤ C*s*distance k u v

end Diamond

/-- The space
is the full continuous dual of the specified segment-norm completion, not the
coordinate completion in place of that dual. -/
theorem main_theorem :
    (¬FiniteDimensional ℝ (FullDual Vertex)) ∧
    TopologicalSpace.SeparableSpace (FullDual Vertex) ∧
    Reflexive (FullDual Vertex) ∧
    (∀ (α β : ℝ) (R : EquivalentNorm (FullDual Vertex) α β), ¬IsAUC R.N) ∧
    (∀ t : ℝ, 0 < t →
      Real.sqrt (1+t^2/12)-1 ≤ averageModulus (FullDual Vertex) norm t) ∧
    (∀ (k : ℕ) (f : (Diamond.diamond k).V → FullDual Vertex) (C : ℝ),
      Diamond.EmbeddingBound k f C → Real.sqrt (1+(k:ℝ)/12) ≤ C) := by
  sorry

end ForestSpace

end

end OAI
```

    "text": "The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):\n\n- [Asymptotic midpoint uniform convexity and unbounded diamond distortion in a reflexive tree space](../../preprints/Asymptotic-midpoint-uniform-convexity-and-unbounded-diamond-distortion-in-a-reflexive-tree-space-September-27-2026/manuscript.pdf)\n- [Midpoint lenses in segment spaces](../../preprints/Midpoint-lenses-in-segment-spaces-September-27-2026/manuscript.pdf)\n- [Distortion of countably branching diamonds from midpoint and tree energies](../../preprints/Diamond-distortion-from-midpoint-and-tree-energies-September-27-2026/manuscript.pdf)\n- [Exact asymptotic moduli in a Daugavet subspace of $L_1$](../../preprints/Exact-asymptotic-moduli-in-a-Daugavet-subspace-of-L1-September-27-2026/manuscript.pdf)\n- [Midpoint convexity from bounded tree potentials and path costs](../../preprints/Midpoint-convexity-from-bounded-tree-potentials-and-path-costs-September-27-2026/manuscript.pdf)\n- [Independent products in real $L_1$: asymptotic midpoint convexity without AUC renormings](../../preprints/Independent-products-in-real-L1-asymptotic-midpoint-convexity-without-AUC-renormings-September-27-2026/manuscript.pdf)\n- [Midpoint convexity from two recursive potentials](../../preprints/Midpoint-convexity-from-two-recursive-potentials-September-27-2026/manuscript.pdf)\n\n## Scope\n\nThe paper constructs a Banach space whose midpoint geometry is asymptotically uniformly convex even though no equivalent norm is asymptotically uniformly convex in the usual one-sided sense. The formalization proves that the full dual of the specified segment-norm completion is infinite-dimensional, separable, and reflexive; its averaged midpoint modulus is at least $\\sqrt{1+t^2/12}-1$ for every $t>0$, while every equivalent norm fails asymptotic uniform convexity. It also proves that an embedding of a depth-$k$ countably branching diamond has distortion at least $\\sqrt{1+k/12}$.\n\nThe general-forest and word-forest results use the closed span of the coordinate functionals. Their conclusions retain the finite-ancestor and equivalent-norm hypotheses, including the scale $\\alpha/(2\\beta)$ when $0<\\alpha\\le\\beta$ and the new norm lies between $\\alpha$ and $\\beta$ times the original norm; they do not identify that span with the full dual or assert reflexivity for every forest.\n\nThe paper controls midpoint lenses in Banach spaces defined by tree-segment norms. The formalization proves the following estimate in both the full dual of the finite-height forest space and the coordinate predual of the infinite-tree space. If $x$ is supported on a finite ancestral set $H$, $R\\ge0$, and $\\|x+y\\|,\\|x-y\\|\\le R$, then the part of $y$ outside $H$ has norm at most $2\\sqrt{R^2-\\|x\\|^2}$.\n\nThe formalization also covers selected consequences for averaged midpoint moduli and separated families of vectors, together with reflexivity and renorming obstructions. This scope concerns the segment-space results; the companion results on diamond energies remain separate.\n\nThe paper studies embeddings of countably branching diamond graphs into Banach spaces built from tree segments. The formalization proves that every embedding of the depth-$k$ diamond with distortion $D$, at any positive scale, satisfies $1+k/4\\le D^2$ in either of two spaces: the full dual of the completed finite-height forest space and the closed coordinate span in the dual of the infinite-tree space. The bound applies to all pairs of vertices and is unconditional.\n\nThe formalization also contains selected graph deductions under explicit companion assumptions. Twenty-two of those companion inputs are assumed in these deductions; the diamond distortion bound above does not require them.\n\nThe paper exhibits a real $L_1$ subspace with positive averaged midpoint convexity but no equivalent asymptotically uniformly convex norm. For every closed infinite-dimensional subspace whose unit ball is precompact in measure and which has the Daugavet property, the formalization computes the moduli at every unit vector: the averaged midpoint modulus is $\\max(t/2,t-1)$ and the usual one-sided asymptotic modulus is $\\max(0,t-2)$ for every $t>0$. The same formulas hold after taking the infimum over unit vectors, and every equivalent norm fails asymptotic uniform convexity.\n\nThe formalization also constructs such a subspace on a countable product of unit intervals and proves that it has the stated measure-precompactness and Daugavet properties, so the modulus formulas apply to an actual example.\n\nThe paper constructs Banach spaces from bounded tree potentials and path costs to separate averaged midpoint convexity from asymptotic uniform convexity. For the specified tree-potential completions, the formalization proves completeness, separability, infinite dimension, and an averaged midpoint modulus of at least $\\sqrt{1+t^2/4}-1$ for $0<t<1$. No space linearly isomorphic to one of these completions is asymptotically uniformly convex.\n\nThe formalization also covers selected path-cost duality and comparison results, clipping and energy estimates, and further renorming obstructions. These retain their stated support, head, and tail hypotheses; the remaining auxiliary assertions of the paper are outside this scope.\n\nThe paper forms a real $L_1$ space from the closed span of products of independent multipliers along tree paths. For a positive, nonconstant multiplier of mean one and finite second moment, the formalization proves that this space is infinite-dimensional and has no equivalent asymptotically uniformly convex norm.\n\nFor every $t>0$, the averaged midpoint modulus nevertheless has explicit positive lower bounds. If $G$ is a standard real Gaussian, the exponential-multiplier bound is $\\frac{7t}{480}\\mathbb E[(|G|-20/t)_+]$, where $u_+=\\max(u,0)$. For squared-Gaussian multipliers, the bounds are $\\frac18\\Pr(|G|\\ge4\\sqrt2(2+\\sqrt2)/t)$ and $\\frac14\\Pr(|G|\\ge8\\sqrt6/t)$.\n\nThe paper constructs three Banach spaces from recursive tree potentials: a root-sum space, a zero-root space, and a joined space. The formalization proves that all three are infinite-dimensional and have no equivalent asymptotically uniformly convex norm. The root-sum and joined spaces have averaged midpoint modulus at least $t^3/128$ for $0<t<1$, and the root-sum modulus is positive for every $t>0$. The zero-root estimate retains its finite ancestral support and tail hypotheses, and the zero-root and joined spaces are reflexive.\n\nThe formalization also covers a separated-midpoint result for the joined space and a variable-exponent model with its recursive norm, duality, projections, renorming obstruction, and sixth- and third-order stability estimates. The separation, sequence, support, and head assumptions in these results are essential parts of their statements."

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We construct a separable reflexive real Banach space whose given norm is asymptotically midpoint uniformly convex but which admits no asymptotically uniformly convex equivalent norm. Its averaged midpoint modulus is at least $`\sqrt{1+t^2/12}-1`$, and the countably branching diamond of depth <i>k</i> has distortion at least $`\sqrt{1+k/12}`$ in this space. This gives a negative answer to the reflexive diamond converse for asymptotic uniform convexifiability.

<a id="pm-h-1"></a>
#### Statements index

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

Asymptotic convexity describes the convexity that remains after finitely many directions are discarded. The question considered here is whether symmetric midpoint convexity can persist in a reflexive Banach space which admits no asymptotically uniformly convex equivalent norm.

<a id="eq:average-modulus"></a>
All spaces and scalars are real. For an infinite-dimensional Banach space $Y$ with norm $N$, write $\operatorname{cof}(Y)$ for the closed finite-codimensional subspaces of $Y$. For $t>0$, define <a id="eq:auc-modulus"></a>$$\begin{aligned}
 \overline\delta_N(t)
 &=\inf_{N(x)=1}\sup_{F\in\operatorname{cof}(Y)}
   \inf_{y\in F,\,N(y)=1}\bigl(N(x+ty)-1\bigr),
   \label{eq:auc-modulus}\\
 \widehat\delta_N(t)
 &=\inf_{N(x)=1}\sup_{F\in\operatorname{cof}(Y)}
   \inf_{y\in F,\,N(y)=1}
   \left(\frac{N(x+ty)+N(x-ty)}2-1\right).
   \label{eq:average-modulus}
\end{aligned}$$ The norm is *asymptotically uniformly convex* (AUC) if $\overline\delta_N(t)>0$ for all $t>0$, and *asymptotically midpoint uniformly convex* (AMUC) if $\widehat\delta_N(t)>0$ for all $t>0$. Thus AUC requires a gain in every sufficiently far-out direction; AMUC permits the two opposite directions to be tested together. A norm $N$ is equivalent to a given norm $\|\cdot\|$ if $a\|x\|\le N(x)\le b\|x\|$ for some $0<a\le b<\infty$ and all $x$. A space is *AUC renormable* if it admits an equivalent AUC norm.

Dilworth, Kutzarova, Randrianarivony, Revalski, and Zhivkov introduced AMUC and constructed an AMUC norm on $\ell_2$ which is not AUC (Dilworth et al. 2016, Definition 2.2 and Theorem 2.4) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[DKRRZ2016](#ref-DKRRZ2016)]. Their question about equivalence up to renorming (Dilworth et al. 2016, sec. 5) <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[DKRRZ2016](#ref-DKRRZ2016)] has already been answered negatively by Baudier (F. P. Baudier 2026, Theorem 1 and Corollary 1) <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[Baudier2026](#ref-Baudier2026)], using the Kadets–Werner modification of the Bourgain–Rosenthal space (Kadets and Werner 2004, Theorem 2.5) <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[KW2004](#ref-KW2004)]. We give a reflexive example with an explicit midpoint estimate.

The historical definition of AMUC uses the maximum of the two signed norms in <a href="#eq:average-modulus" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:average-modulus">[eq:average-modulus]</a>. This gives the same positivity property. The average never exceeds the maximum. Conversely, intersect any chosen finite-codimensional subspace with the kernel of a functional norming $x$. Both signed norms are then at least one, so a gap for their maximum gives half that gap for their average. Our quantitative modulus always uses the average in <a href="#eq:average-modulus" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:average-modulus">[eq:average-modulus]</a>.

<a id="eq:embedding-definition"></a>
The example also addresses a metric question. Define the *countably branching diamonds* by taking $D_0$ to be a single edge and replacing every edge at each step by countably many internally disjoint two-edge paths. Each current edge has length one; let $d_k$ be the shortest-path metric on $D_k$. An embedding of $D_k$ into a Banach space has distortion at most $D$ if it is a map $f$ for which some scale $s>0$ satisfies $$\label{eq:embedding-definition}
 s\,d_k(u,v)\le\|f(u)-f(v)\|\le Ds\,d_k(u,v)
 \qquad(u,v\in D_k).$$ The family embeds uniformly when one finite $D$ works for all $k$. For finite binary diamonds, Lee and Naor obtained quantitative $L_p$ distortion bounds by summing convexity losses (Lee and Naor 2004, Lemma 2.2) <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[LeeNaor2004](#ref-LeeNaor2004)], and Johnson and Schechtman developed a self-improvement proof (Johnson and Schechtman 2009, sec. 4) <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[JohnsonSchechtman2009](#ref-JohnsonSchechtman2009)]. Baudier, Causey, Dilworth, Kutzarova, Randrianarivony, Schlumprecht, and Zhang proved that an equivalent AMUC norm prevents uniform embeddings of the countably branching diamonds (F. Baudier et al. 2017, Theorem 4.1) <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[BaudierEtAl2017](#ref-BaudierEtAl2017)]. For reflexive spaces with unconditional asymptotic structure, they also proved that failure of AUC renormability implies uniform diamond embeddings (F. Baudier et al. 2017, Theorem B and Corollary 5.3) <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[BaudierEtAl2017](#ref-BaudierEtAl2017)]. Swift extended the embedding construction to countably branching bundle graphs under the same reflexive, unconditional, and non-AUC-renormable hypotheses (Swift 2018, Theorem 3.2 and Corollary 3.4) <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[Swift2018](#ref-Swift2018)]. Perreau subsequently treated duals of separable spaces with weak-star unconditional asymptotic structure, retaining unconditional asymptotic structure in the reflexive formulation (Perreau 2021, Theorems 4.8 and 5.3) <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[Perreau2021](#ref-Perreau2021)]. The example below shows that the diamond implication fails for general reflexive spaces.

More recently, Basset, Lancien, and Procházka related AMUC to separated families of midpoints and obtained the diamond obstruction even when the maps are controlled only on specified vertex pairs (Basset, Lancien, and Procházka 2026, Definition 6.15 and Propositions 6.16 and 6.20) <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[BassetLancienProchazka2026](#ref-BassetLancienProchazka2026)]. Malthaner obtained quantitative obstructions for countably branching diamonds using a Matoušek-type coloring and path argument (Malthaner 2025, Theorems 3.5 and 3.7) <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[Malthaner2025](#ref-Malthaner2025)]. We use the full bi-Lipschitz convention <a href="#eq:embedding-definition" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:embedding-definition">[eq:embedding-definition]</a> and prove the required midpoint estimate directly.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### The example and result

<a id="eq:segment-norm"></a>
For each integer $h\ge1$, let $T_h=\mathbb N^{\le h}$ be the rooted tree of words of length at most $h$, ordered by extension of words. Its root is the empty word. Keep separate copies of these trees in the forest $$\mathcal F=\coprod_{h\ge1}(\{h\}\times T_h).$$ Write $a\preceq b$ if $a$ is an ancestor of $b$ in the same component. A *segment* is a nonempty interval $[a,b]=\{v:a\preceq v\preceq b\}$ in an ancestral chain. For a finitely supported real function $u$ on $\mathcal F$, set $u(S)=\sum_{v\in S}u(v)$ and define $$\label{eq:segment-norm}
 \rho(u)=\sup\left\{\left(\sum_{i=1}^m u(S_i)^2\right)^{1/2}:
  m\ge1,\ S_1,\ldots,S_m\text{ pairwise disjoint segments}\right\}.$$ The Euclidean triangle inequality and singleton segments show that $\rho$ is a norm, with $\|u\|_2\le\rho(u)\le\|u\|_1$. Let $J$ be its completion and let $X=J^*$, with the dual norm denoted by $\|\cdot\|$. We write $e_v\in J$ for a coordinate unit vector. Singleton tests give $|u(v)|\le\rho(u)$, so coordinate evaluation extends to an element $e_v^*\in X$. The construction in <a href="#eq:segment-norm" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:segment-norm">[eq:segment-norm]</a> uses James’s square-sum norm on disjoint segments (James 1974, 739) <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[James1974](#ref-James1974)]; the branching and component heights here differ from the classical binary tree.

For the classical binary James tree space, Girardi proved AUC of both the coordinate predual and the full dual (Girardi 2001, Theorems 3 and 5) <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[Girardi2001](#ref-Girardi2001)]. The countable branching and finite, unbounded component heights used here lead to the following different behavior.

<a id="stmt-thm-main"></a>
<div id="thm:main" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1**. *The space $X=J^*$ defined by <a href="#eq:segment-norm" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:segment-norm">[eq:segment-norm]</a> is infinite dimensional, separable, and reflexive, and has no equivalent AUC norm. Its given norm satisfies $$\widehat\delta_X(t)\ge\sqrt{1+t^2/12}-1\qquad(t>0).$$ For every integer $k\ge0$, every embedding of $D_k$ into $X$ has distortion at least $\sqrt{1+k/12}$.*

</div>

This gives a negative answer to the reflexive diamond question recorded as Problem 39 in (F. P. Baudier and Lancien 2026, author manuscript p. 388) <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[BaudierLancien2025](#ref-BaudierLancien2025)]. The metric obstruction is unchanged by equivalent renorming, although the numerical constants refer to the specified dual norm.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### How the argument fits together

Section <a href="#sec:forest" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:forest">2</a> establishes reflexivity and the coordinate projections used throughout the proof. A sum of coordinate functionals along one root path has norm one, while its next coordinate increment is a weakly null unit vector as the child varies. In Section <a href="#sec:no-auc" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:no-auc">3</a>, a hypothetical AUC norm forces repeated multiplicative gains along one such path. Arbitrarily high finite trees contradict its bounded norm.

The midpoint argument in Section <a href="#sec:paired" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:paired">4</a> must control segment tests crossing from a finite ancestral set (one closed under taking ancestors) to its complement. Simply adding norming tests for the two parts creates uncontrolled positive cross terms. We add one correction at their first exit vertices. The correction makes both resulting tests inexpensive, and its evaluations cancel when the two endpoint norms are averaged. Lemma <a href="#lem:paired" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:paired">6</a> expresses the result as a lower bound in terms of the squared head and tail norms. Section <a href="#sec:general-forest" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:general-forest">4.1</a> extends this estimate to coordinate completions on general rooted forests.

Finally, the infinitely many middle vertices of a diamond supply a separated family of approximate midpoints. Two of their finite coordinate projections can be made arbitrarily close. Their half-difference therefore reduces the metric problem to the head–tail estimate. Section <a href="#sec:diamonds" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:diamonds">5</a> turns this into a loss in squared stretch at every replacement and sums the losses. This gives a direct quantitative instance of the midpoint and self-improvement methods for these graphs (F. Baudier et al. 2017, sec. 4) <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[BaudierEtAl2017](#ref-BaudierEtAl2017)].

<a id="sec:forest"></a>
### The coordinate structure of the forest

We first establish the structural facts needed to separate a vector into finitely many coordinates and its complement. The finite heights give reflexivity and coordinate density; the ancestral order gives contractive projections and an exact norm formula on the complementary subtrees.

For a finite set $H$, the coordinate projections are $P_Hu=\sum_{v\in H}u(v)e_v$ on $J$ and $P_Hx=\sum_{v\in H}x(e_v)e_v^*$ on $X$; write $Q_H=I-P_H$.

<a id="stmt-lem-forest-structure"></a>
<div id="lem:forest-structure" class="lemma">

**Lemma 2**. *The space $X$ defined by <a href="#eq:segment-norm" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:segment-norm">[eq:segment-norm]</a> is infinite dimensional, separable, and reflexive, and the coordinate functionals have dense span in $X$. Let $H\subset\mathcal F$ be finite and *ancestral*, meaning that it contains every ancestor of each of its vertices. The coordinate projections $P_H$ and $Q_H$ are contractive on both $J$ and $X$. Moreover, $P_Hx\to x$ along any increasing ancestral exhaustion of $\mathcal F$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-forest-structure"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-forest-structure</summary>

*Proof.* Singleton tests give $\|u\|_2\le\rho(u)$. On a component of height $h$, each segment has at most $h+1$ vertices. Thus Cauchy–Schwarz and disjointness give $$\|u\|_2\le\rho(u)\le\sqrt{h+1}\|u\|_2.$$ Write $J_h$ for the completion on that component. Segment families from different components combine freely, so $$J=\left(\bigoplus_{h\ge1}J_h\right)_{\ell_2}.$$ Each $J_h$ is isomorphic to Hilbert space. The dual of an $\ell_2$ sum is isometrically the $\ell_2$ sum of the duals: Cauchy–Schwarz proves one inequality, and testing on almost norming unit vectors in finitely many components, with proportional weights, proves the other. Applying this identity twice proves reflexivity. In particular $X^*=J$ canonically. If the coordinate functionals did not have dense span in $X$, Hahn–Banach would give a nonzero element of $X^*=J$ annihilating them. All its coordinates would vanish, a contradiction. Countability of $\mathcal F$ now proves separability; the independent coordinates prove infinite dimensionality.

A segment meets $H$ in a prefix and its complement in a suffix. Restricting disjoint segment tests to either set therefore proves contractivity on $J$. The adjoints give precisely the coordinate projections on $X$, and remain contractive. Finally, approximate $x$ by a finite coordinate combination. Once $H$ contains its support, contractivity bounds the discarded tail of $x$ by that approximation error. This proves convergence along an ancestral exhaustion. ◻

</details>

In particular, the completion of the finite coordinate functionals in $$\|x\|=\sup\left\{\sum_vx(v)u(v):u\in c_{00}(\mathcal F),\ \rho(u)\le1\right\}$$ is exactly $X$, under the coordinate pairing. This identifies the dual and coordinate-completion descriptions of the present finite-height forest. Each component has infinitely many vertices; only its height is finite, and no Hilbert equivalence constant uniform in the height was needed.

For $x=P_Hx$, restriction to $P_HJ$ preserves its functional norm. The finite-dimensional unit ball is compact, so there is $f=P_Hf$ with $\rho(f)\le1$ and $x(f)=\|x\|$. If $x\ne0$ then $\rho(f)=1$; if $x=0$, including when $H$ is empty, take $f=0$.

Let $G_H$ be the set of minimal vertices outside $H$. We call these vertices *gates*. They include roots of components disjoint from $H$. Distinct gates are incomparable, and the descendant sets $U_g=\{v:g\preceq v\}$ partition $\mathcal F\setminus H$. Each segment crossing from $H$ to its complement passes through one gate. Figure <a href="#fig:forest-gates" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="fig:forest-gates">1</a> illustrates the partition.

<figure id="fig:forest-gates">

<figcaption>A finite ancestor-closed head and some of its gates. Black large nodes belong to $H$; circled nodes are gates. Shading indicates two complementary descendant cones. Only finitely many of the countably many branches are drawn. In particular an untouched component begins with a gate at its root.</figcaption>
</figure>

Let $J(U_g)$ denote the coordinate subspace of $J$ supported in $U_g$, with its inherited norm.

<a id="stmt-lem-cones"></a>
<div id="lem:cones" class="lemma">

**Lemma 3**. *For every finite ancestral $H$, <a id="eq:cone-decomposition"></a>$$\label{eq:cone-decomposition}
 Q_HJ=\left(\bigoplus_{g\in G_H}J(U_g)\right)_{\ell_2},
 \qquad
 \|Q_Hx\|^2=\sum_{g\in G_H}\|x|_{J(U_g)}\|^2\quad(x\in X).$$ The gate-coordinate map $x\mapsto(x(e_g))_{g\in G_H}$ is a contraction from $X$ to $\ell_2(G_H)$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-cones"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-cones</summary>

*Proof.* On a vector supported outside $H$, every segment test reduces to a segment in one cone. Grouping a disjoint family by its gates gives the upper estimate for the first identity; combining almost maximizing families from finitely many distinct cones gives the reverse estimate. Pass to the completion. A functional supported outside $H$ has exactly the norm of its restriction to $Q_HJ$, because $Q_H$ is contractive. Duality for the $\ell_2$ sum therefore proves the second identity.

A segment contains at most one gate, so $\rho(u)=\|u\|_2$ for vectors supported on $G_H$. Testing $x$ on such finite vectors proves the gate-coordinate contraction. ◻

</details>

<a id="eq:antichain-isometry"></a>
If $A$ is any antichain and $(a_v)_{v\in A}$ has finite support, then $$\label{eq:antichain-isometry}
 \rho\left(\sum_{v\in A}a_ve_v\right)
 =\left\|\sum_{v\in A}a_ve_v^*\right\|
 =\left(\sum_{v\in A}a_v^2\right)^{1/2}.$$ The first equality with the Euclidean norm follows because a segment meets $A$ at most once. For the dual norm, singleton tests give the upper bound, and testing on the proportional vector supported on $A$ gives the lower bound. Thus these identities concern the specified norm, not merely equivalent norms on the same coordinate space.

<a id="sec:no-auc"></a>
### Why no equivalent norm is AUC

The renorming obstruction uses bounded root paths and weakly null child increments. We first isolate the elementary finite-codimensional argument; its midpoint counterpart will be proved separately.

<a id="stmt-lem-modulus-conventions"></a>
<div id="lem:modulus-conventions" class="lemma">

**Lemma 4** (Modulus conventions). *For an infinite-dimensional real Banach space $Y$ with norm $N$, let $m_N$ be the modulus obtained from <a href="#eq:average-modulus" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:average-modulus">[eq:average-modulus]</a> by replacing the average by $\max\{N(x+ty),N(x-ty)\}$. Then, for $t>0$, $$0\le\overline\delta_N(t)\le\widehat\delta_N(t)
 \le m_N(t)\le2\widehat\delta_N(t).$$ For either symmetric modulus the inner infimum is unchanged if $N(y)=1$ is replaced by $N(y)\ge1$. Both symmetric moduli, including their centerwise versions before taking the infimum over $x$, are nondecreasing. Positivity on $(0,1)$ therefore implies positivity at every positive radius.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-modulus-conventions"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-modulus-conventions</summary>

*Proof.* For fixed $x,y$, the average and maximum of the two signed norms are even convex functions of the radius, hence nondecreasing on $[0,\infty)$. This proves the assertions about radii and monotonicity. A norming functional for a unit center $x$ makes both signed norms at least one on its kernel. Intersecting any chosen finite-codimensional subspace with this kernel proves nonnegativity of the one-sided modulus and that the maximum excess is at most twice the average excess, after taking the supremum over subspaces. The average is pointwise at most the maximum. Finally, on a fixed subspace the infimum of the one-sided excess is a lower bound for each of the two signed excesses, since $y$ and $-y$ both belong to that subspace. Taking the successive suprema and infima proves the remaining comparison. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-lem-bounded-tree"></a>
<div id="lem:bounded-tree" class="lemma">

<a id="eq:bounded-tree-scale"></a>
**Lemma 5** (Bounded trees). *Let $Y$ be a real Banach space. Suppose that for every integer $h\ge1$ there are vectors $(u_s^{(h)})_{s\in\mathbb N^{\le h}}$ and constants $m,M,c>0$, independent of $h$, such that $$m\le\|u_s^{(h)}\|\le M,
 \qquad
 \|u_{s^\frown j}^{(h)}-u_s^{(h)}\|\ge c\quad(|s|<h).$$ For every nonterminal $s$, suppose the child increments tend weakly to zero as $j\to\infty$. Then every equivalent norm satisfying $\alpha\|\cdot\|\le N\le\beta\|\cdot\|$, with $0<\alpha\le\beta<\infty$, obeys $$\label{eq:bounded-tree-scale}
 \overline\delta_N\!\left(\frac{\alpha c}{2\beta M}\right)=0.$$ The same conclusion holds for one infinite tree with these properties.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-bounded-tree"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-bounded-tree</summary>

*Proof.* Put $t_0=\alpha c/(2\beta M)$. If the displayed modulus is positive, choose $0<\gamma<\overline\delta_N(t_0)$. At a current vector $u=u_s^{(h)}$, the definition supplies a finite-codimensional $F$ such that $$N(u+t_0N(u)y)\ge(1+\gamma)N(u)
 \qquad(y\in F,\ N(y)=1).$$ Convexity extends this to every $w\in F$ with $N(w)\ge t_0N(u)$: for a unit center $z$ and $r\ge t_0$, $$N(z+ry)-1\ge\frac r{t_0}\bigl(N(z+t_0y)-1\bigr).$$ The weakly null increments $d_j=u_{s^\frown j}^{(h)}-u$ have images tending to zero in norm in the finite-dimensional quotient $Y/F$. Thus there are $w_j\in F$ with $N(w_j-d_j)\to0$. Eventually $N(w_j)\ge\alpha c/2\ge t_0N(u)$. Taking the approximation error at most $\gamma N(u)/2$ gives a child with $$N(u_{s^\frown j}^{(h)})\ge(1+\gamma/2)N(u_s^{(h)}).$$ The gain is uniform although $F$ may depend on the current node. Choose $h$ with $\alpha m(1+\gamma/2)^h>\beta M$ and make this choice successively along a branch of that tree. The resulting norm exceeds $\beta M$, a contradiction. The increments in particular force $Y$ to be infinite dimensional, and Lemma <a href="#lem:modulus-conventions" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:modulus-conventions">4</a> gives the reverse nonnegative inequality in <a href="#eq:bounded-tree-scale" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:bounded-tree-scale">[eq:bounded-tree-scale]</a>. An infinite tree may be restricted to any sufficiently large height. ◻

</details>

We apply the lemma to the forest. For a node $v$ in the component with root $r$, put $p_v=\sum_{r\preceq w\preceq v}e_w^*$. The root path is one segment test, so $\|p_v\|\le1$; evaluation at a coordinate on that path gives equality. At a nonterminal node the child increments are $e_{v^\frown j}^*$, each of norm one. They tend weakly to zero: $X^*=J$, and every $u\in J$ has square-summable coordinates, so $u(v^\frown j)\to0$. We therefore have $m=M=c=1$ in Lemma <a href="#lem:bounded-tree" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:bounded-tree">5</a>. For every equivalent norm $\alpha\|\cdot\|\le N\le\beta\|\cdot\|$, $$\overline\delta_N(\alpha/(2\beta))=0.$$ This proves the renorming assertion in Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>.

<a id="sec:paired"></a>
### A paired extension of segment tests

The renorming obstruction is proved. To establish midpoint convexity, we now show that a symmetric pair must grow when its center and displacement occupy a finite ancestral set and its complement. The squared-norm form will also be the input for the diamond argument.

<a id="stmt-lem-paired"></a>
<div id="lem:paired" class="lemma">

<a id="eq:paired"></a>
<a id="eq:paired-two-parameters"></a>
**Lemma 6**. *Let $H\subset\mathcal F$ be finite and ancestral. If $x,y\in X$ satisfy $x=P_Hx$ and $y=Q_Hy$, then $$\label{eq:paired}
 \frac{\|x+y\|+\|x-y\|}{2}
 \ge\sqrt{\|x\|^2+\|y\|^2/12}.$$ More generally, for all $s,t>0$, $$\label{eq:paired-two-parameters}
 \frac{\|x+ty\|+\|x-ty\|}{2}
 \ge\frac{\|x\|+st\|y\|}{\sqrt{1+12s^2}}.$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-paired"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-paired</summary>

<a id="eq:paired-parameter"></a>
*Proof.* We first prove, for every $s>0$, $$\label{eq:paired-parameter}
 \frac{\|x+y\|+\|x-y\|}{2}
 \ge\frac{\|x\|+s\|y\|}{\sqrt{1+12s^2}}.$$ Suppose initially that $y$ has finite support. Choose finite tests $f,k\in J$, supported in $H$ and its complement respectively, with $\rho(f),\rho(k)\le1$ and pairings as close as desired to $\|x\|$ and $\|y\|$. The naive tests $f+sk$ and $f-sk$ can have large positive crossing terms. We will add the same vector to both tail tests; its contribution to the endpoint average will cancel. To control its size, at each gate $g$ put $$m_g=\sup_{w\succeq g}|k([g,w])|.$$ Only finitely many $m_g$ are nonzero, and $\sum_gm_g^2\le1$: choose an almost maximizing initial segment in each of finitely many distinct cones and use their disjointness.

For a gate with parent $v\in H$, consider the suffixes $[a,v]$ whose $f$-sum has magnitude at least $2sm_g$. If $m_g>0$ and such suffixes exist, choose the shortest one and let $\sigma_g$ be opposite to the sign of its sum. In all other cases put $\sigma_g=1$. Set $$c=\sum_g\sigma_gm_ge_g,\qquad v^+=c+k,\qquad v^-=c-k.$$ The gates form an antichain, so $\rho(c)\le1$ and $\rho(v^\pm)\le2$. Every initial segment in the cone of $g$ has $v^\pm$-sum of sign $\sigma_g$ or zero, and magnitude at most $2m_g$.

We claim $\rho(f+sv^\pm)^2\le1+12s^2$. Fix a disjoint family of segments and denote their $f$- and $v^\pm$-sums by $\alpha_i$ and $\beta_i$. We have $\sum_i\beta_i^2\le4$. A positive cross term $\alpha_i\beta_i$ can occur only at a crossing segment, with some gate $g$; different crossing segments use different gates. If $|\alpha_i|\le2sm_g$, then $2s\alpha_i\beta_i\le8s^2m_g^2$. Otherwise the head itself reaches the threshold, so it contains the shortest selected suffix. Write $\alpha'_i$ for that suffix’s $f$-sum. Since $\alpha_i\beta_i>0$ and $\beta_i$ has sign $\sigma_g$, the choice of $\sigma_g$ gives $\alpha_i\alpha'_i<0$. Thus the selected suffix is proper, and $|\alpha'_i|\ge2sm_g$ by its selection. Splitting it off the head increases the sum of squared $f$-sums by $$(\alpha_i-\alpha'_i)^2+(\alpha'_i)^2-\alpha_i^2
 =2(\alpha'_i)^2+2|\alpha_i\alpha'_i|
 \ge4s|\alpha_i|m_g\ge2s\alpha_i\beta_i.$$ Figure <a href="#fig:paired-cut" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="fig:paired-cut">2</a> shows the proper suffix and the cut, with the segment index suppressed.

<figure id="fig:paired-cut">

<figcaption>The threshold cut for a crossing segment. Its head has $f$-sum $α$ and its selected suffix has $f$-sum $α′$. When $αβ > 0$ and $|α| > 2sm_{g}$, the sign choice gives $αα′ < 0$ and $|α′| ≥ 2sm_{g}$. Cutting immediately before $a′$ therefore increases the squared $f$-test by at least $2sαβ$. The vertex spacing is schematic.</figcaption>
</figure>

<a id="pm-figsrc-fig-paired-cut"></a>
**Figure source and visual witness** (`fig:paired-cut`)

 \draw[thick] (0,0)--(9.2,0);
 \draw[dashed,gray] (6.25,-.7)--(6.25,.8);
 \foreach \x in {0,1,2,3,4,5,6,6.6,7.5,8.3,9.2}
   \fill (\x,0) circle (1.7pt);
 \node[below] at (0,0) {$a$};
 \node[below] at (4,0) {$a'$};
 \node[below] at (6,0) {$v$};
 \node[below] at (6.6,0) {$g$};
 \node[below] at (9.2,0) {$w$};
 \draw[decorate,decoration={brace,amplitude=4pt}] (0,.35)--(6,.35)
   node[midway,above=5pt] {head sum $\alpha$};
 \draw[decorate,decoration={brace,amplitude=4pt}] (4,.95)--(6,.95)
   node[midway,above=5pt] {selected suffix $\alpha'$};
 \draw[decorate,decoration={brace,amplitude=4pt}] (6.6,.35)--(9.2,.35)
   node[midway,above=5pt] {tail sum $\beta$};
